## Saved state (N1): `persistent(initial, "key")` is a `state(...)` that the app finds again
## the next time it runs. Values are packed like hot-reload snapshots (hotstate.nim) and kept
## by the host in the app's own folder (host.nim `storeSet`: 1 MB a value, 16 MB an app).

import runtime, signals, hotstate

when sizeof(pointer) == 4:                # wasm: hand the host (pointer, length) pairs
  proc ptrOf(s: string): int32 = (if s.len == 0: 0'i32 else: cast[int32](cast[uint](unsafeAddr s[0])))

  proc loadStored*(key: string; into: var string): bool =
    ## The bytes saved under `key`, if any.
    let n = yStoreGet(ptrOf(key), int32(key.len), 0, 0)
    if n < 0: return false
    into = newString(int(n))
    if n > 0: discard yStoreGet(ptrOf(key), int32(key.len), ptrOf(into), n)
    true

  proc saveStored*(key, bytes: string): bool =
    ## Saves bytes under `key`; false if the host refused (bad key, too big, over quota).
    yStoreSet(ptrOf(key), int32(key.len), ptrOf(bytes), int32(bytes.len)) == 1
else:                                     # native builds (tests) have no host: a test sets these
  var nativeStoreGet*: proc (key: string): (bool, string)
  var nativeStoreSet*: proc (key, bytes: string): bool

  proc loadStored*(key: string; into: var string): bool =
    if nativeStoreGet == nil: return false
    let (found, v) = nativeStoreGet(key)
    if found: into = v
    found

  proc saveStored*(key, bytes: string): bool =
    nativeStoreSet != nil and nativeStoreSet(key, bytes)

proc persistent*[T](initial: T; key: string): Signal[T] =  ## A signal holding `initial` the first time, and whatever it last held ever after.
  ## Every change is saved at once. A saved value that no longer fits the type (the app
  ## changed) is ignored.
  var start = initial
  var saved: string
  if loadStored(key, saved):
    var v: T
    var p = 0
    if unpackFrom(saved, p, v) and p == saved.len: start = v
  result = newState(start)
  let s = result
  discard createEffect(proc () =
    var b = ""
    packInto(b, s.get())
    discard saveStored(key, b))

proc storeGet*(namespace, key: string): string =
  ## Cross-module string slot backed by the host store (yStoreGet).
  ## NOTE: the host keeps one store per app folder, so "desk" slots are only
  ## shared inside the SAME app instance. Native devwatch bridges
  ## liveedit.<id> / buildstatus.<id> across apps on disk.
  var v = ""
  if loadStored(namespace & "." & key, v): v else: ""

proc storeSet*(namespace, key, value: string) =
  ## Saves a cross-module string slot (yStoreSet). See storeGet note.
  discard saveStored(namespace & "." & key, value)


================================================================
FILE: sdk/core/ai.nim
================================================================
## AI: ask a language model. The app never holds a key: the host adds it (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY,
## read from its environment, never stored) or talks to a local model server (YANTRA_AI_URL,
## any OpenAI-compatible one: llama.cpp, Ollama). The app must declare `needs("ai")`.
##   aiAsk(@[AiTurn(role: "user", text: "Hi")], "You are…", proc (ok: bool; text: string) = …)

import std/json
import runtime

type AiTurn* = object
  role*: string               ## "user" or "assistant"
  text*: string

proc yAi(req, reqLen, handler: int32): int32 {.importc: "y_ai", cdecl.}
proc yAiReady(): int32 {.importc: "y_ai_ready", cdecl.}

proc aiReady*(): int =
  ## 0: no model is set up on this machine, 1: a hosted model (Claude), 2: a local model server.
  int(yAiReady())

proc answerText(body: string): (bool, string) =
  ## The text of a model's answer, from either API's shape; (false, why) if there is none.
  try:
    let j = parseJson(body)
    if j.hasKey("content"):                              # Anthropic Messages API
      var t = ""
      for part in j["content"]:
        if part{"type"}.getStr == "text": t.add part["text"].getStr
      return (true, t)
    if j.hasKey("choices") and j["choices"].len > 0:     # OpenAI-compatible (local servers)
      return (true, j["choices"][0]{"message"}{"content"}.getStr)
    if j.hasKey("error"):
      return (false, j["error"]{"message"}.getStr(j["error"].getStr))
    (false, "the model's answer had no text")
  except CatchableError:
    (false, "the model's answer could not be read")

proc aiAsk*(turns: seq[AiTurn]; system: string; done: proc (ok: bool; text: string); maxTokens = 1024): bool =
  ## Sends the conversation; `done` gets the answer (or why there is none). False if the host
  ## refused at once: no model set up, the app lacks the `ai` grant, or too many requests.
  var msgs = newJArray()
  for t in turns: msgs.add %*{"role": t.role, "content": t.text}
  let req = $(%*{"system": system, "messages": msgs, "max_tokens": maxTokens})
  var id = new int32
  let h = onHandler(proc () =
    let n = yFetchBody(id[], 0, 0)
    var body = newString(max(n, 0))
    if n > 0: discard yFetchBody(id[], cast[int32](cast[uint](addr body[0])), n)
    let st = yFetchStatus(id[])
    if st >= 200 and st < 300:
      let (ok, text) = answerText(body)
      done(ok, text)
    elif st > 0:
      let (_, why) = answerText(body)
      done(false, "the model service answered " & $st & (if why.len > 0: ": " & why else: ""))
    else:
      done(false, if body.len > 0: body else: "no answer from the model"))
  id[] = yAi(cast[int32](cast[uint](unsafeAddr req[0])), int32(req.len), h)
  id[] != 0


================================================================
FILE: sdk/core/sysinfo.nim
================================================================
## The system: battery, volume, network — what a control centre shows. The app must declare
## `needs("system")`. Every reading is -1 (or "") when this machine does not have or tell it:
## show nothing then, never a made-up value. Windows and Linux (host/sys_win.c, sys_linux.c).

import runtime

proc ySys(op, arg: int32): int32 {.importc: "y_sys", cdecl.}
proc ySysText(op, buf, cap: int32): int32 {.importc: "y_sys_text", cdecl.}

type NetKind* = enum
  netUnknown = -1, netOffline = 0, netWired = 1, netWifi = 2

proc battery*(): int = int(ySys(1, 0))            ## percent 0-100, or -1 (no battery)
proc charging*(): int = int(ySys(2, 0))           ## 1 on mains power, 0 on battery, -1 unknown
proc volume*(): int = int(ySys(3, 0))             ## the output's volume 0-100, or -1
proc setVolume*(pct: int): bool = ySys(4, int32(clamp(pct, 0, 100))) == 1
proc muted*(): int = int(ySys(5, 0))              ## 1 muted, 0 not, -1 unknown
proc setMuted*(on: bool): bool = ySys(6, int32(ord(on))) == 1
proc netKind*(): NetKind =
  let v = ySys(7, 0)
  if v in 0'i32 .. 2'i32: NetKind(v) else: netUnknown

proc sysText(op: int32): string =
  let n = ySysText(op, 0, 0)
  if n <= 0: return ""
  result = newString(n)
  discard ySysText(op, cast[int32](cast[uint](addr result[0])), n)

proc wifiName*(): string = sysText(1)   ## the connected Wi-Fi network's name, or ""
proc userName*(): string = sysText(2)   ## who is signed in to this computer, or ""
proc hostName*(): string = sysText(3)   ## this computer's name, or ""
proc osName*(): string = sysText(4)     ## "Windows", or the Linux distribution's name, or ""

proc uptime*(): int = int(ySys(8, 0))       ## seconds since the machine started, or -1
proc cpuUse*(): int = int(ySys(9, 0))       ## CPU use 0-100 since the last reading, or -1
proc memoryUse*(): int = int(ySys(10, 0))   ## memory in use 0-100, or -1
proc diskUse*(): int = int(ySys(11, 0))     ## the system disk's use 0-100, or -1


================================================================
FILE: sdk/panicoverride.nim
================================================================
## Required by Nim's --os:standalone: the freestanding target has no libc, so the
## compiler demands you supply these two hooks yourself. This is the empirical
## answer to "is Nim->wasm a supported first-class target?" - it compiles, but YOU
## are the runtime. That is precisely the shape an embedded app sandbox needs, and
## precisely why it is not a "free" target.

proc panic(msg: string) {.nimcall.} =
  ## Host-side trap. In a real Yantra app module this would call the host's
  ## `yantra_panic` import so the engine can attribute the fault to this module.
  discard msg

proc rawoutput(msg: string) {.nimcall.} =
  ## stderr equivalent. No filesystem, no console: drop it, or forward to the host.
  discard msg
